**Focus mix of active project-weeks: Data 35%, DevOps 22%, Marketing 17%, Process/Ops 17%, A…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[pe_submerged_code]]

- kind: number · used by: V03

- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 4 Text 103-120 · "Data(1) 35% | DevOps 22% | Marketing 17% | Process / Ops 17% | Admin 6% | Other 3% ... (1) Sales analysis are merged into "Data""
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)

Two scope points missing: footnote (1) in Text 120 says '(1) Sales analysis are merged into Data'; and the per-category slides of the same deck show different figures (SLIDE 6 'Data/Sales/RevOps engine' = '40% of tracked project-weeks'; SLIDE 8 Marketing = '8%*'), so the 35/22/17/17/6/3 split is specifically the slide-4 mix. → Focus mix per slide 4 of aya-work2026.pptx (share of active project-weeks, Jan-Sep 2026): Data 35% (sales analysis merged into Data), DevOps 22%, Marketing 17%, Process/Ops 17%, Admin 6%, Other 3%. Note: the deck's own per-category slides quote Data at 40% and Marketing at 8%*, so cite slide 4 as the source of this split. [caveat] Slide 4 figures sum to 100 (35+22+17+17+6+3) and are verbatim in the source pptx. But the deck is internally inconsistent: slide 6 states Data = '40% of tracked project-weeks' and slide 8 states Marketing = '8%*' (asterisk unexplained); summing the per-category slides gives 40+22+8+17+9 = 96. Slide 4 carries footnote '(1) Sales analysis are merged into Data'. V03 may use it as context only; it does not by itself demonstrate submerged code work. → Focus mix of active project-weeks (slide 4, sums to 100%): Data 35%, DevOps 22%, Marketing 17%, Process/Ops 17%, Admin 6%, Other 3%. Note: the per-category slides of the same deck give Data 40% and Marketing 8%*, so quote slide 4 only and tag it 'self-reported, deck-internal variance'.

## C003

**Code share of active project-weeks grew 41% (Q1) to 53% (Q3); the last reported week (14 S…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[pe_submerged_code]]

- kind: number · used by: V03
- claim: Code share of active project-weeks grew 41% (Q1) to 53% (Q3); the last reported week (14 Sep) reached 63% code; weekly code share rose from 34% (12 Jan).
- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 3 Text 12-16 · "the last reported week (14 Sep) reached 63% code ... Code grew from 41% to 53%"
- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 3 NOTES · "Per quarter, manual/UI, code, people shares of active project-weeks: Q1 36/41/23, Q2 31/50/19, Q3 33/53/14. Weekly code share rises from 34% (12 Jan) to 63% (14 Sep)."
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] Quotes verified in source pptx slide 3 text and notes. Arithmetic: quarterly splits sum to 100 (36/41/23, 31/50/19, 33/53/14). Q3 code:manual = 53/33 = 1.61 matches '1.6:1', but Q1 = 41/36 = 1.14, which the deck headline rounds to '1.2:1' (should be 1.1:1). The 41%/53% are shares of a THREE-way split (manual/UI, code, people), not code-vs-manual; code vs manual only would be 53% -> 62%. The weekly 34% (12 Jan) and 63% (14 Sep) come from the notes and cannot be recomputed (weekly files not admissible). → Code share of active project-weeks (manual/UI / code / people split) rose from 41% in Q1 to 53% in Q3; the last reported week (14 Sep) was 63% code, versus 34% on 12 Jan (deck notes, not recomputable). Avoid the deck's '1.2:1' Q1 ratio: 41/36 = 1.1:1.

## C006

**Ic'Alps merger: 9 months of preparation, 4 days to load the full data in production (mid-M…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[p_ic_load]]

- kind: number · used by: V04, V10
- claim: Ic'Alps merger: 9 months of preparation, 4 days to load the full data in production (mid-May), about 6 weeks of active follow-up.
- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 5 Text 12-16, TextBox 22 · "9 | Month as prep ... 4 Days to load the full data in production ... 6 weeks as active follow-up post-merge* ... *not precise, but there was enhanced support for at least 1,5 month after the merge"
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] SLIDE 5: Text 13 '9 | Month as prep'; Text 15-16 '4' 'Days to load the full data in production'; Text 13-14 '6' 'weeks as active follow-up post-merge*'; Text 12 'Act1. final extract loaded in mid-May'. All numbers present. But the asterisk footnote TextBox 22 reads '*not precise, but there was enhanced support for at least 1,5 month after the merge', so the 6-week figure is explicitly flagged as imprecise by the source. → Ic'Alps merger: 9 months prep, 4 days to load the full data in production (final extract loaded mid-May), ~6 weeks active follow-up post-merge (the deck marks this figure '*not precise, but there was enhanced support for at least 1,5 month after the merge'). [caveat] All three numbers verbatim on slide 5 of the source pptx; '4 days' and 'mid-May' verified. Date arithmetic: 9 months before mid-May 2026 is mid-August 2025, i.e. the prep period starts about 5 months before the first of the 21 weekly reports (12 Jan 2026), so it is self-reported, not derived from the reports. The '6 weeks' carries the deck's own footnote '*not precise, but there was enhanced support for at least 1,5 month' (1.5 months is about 6.5 weeks). → Ic'Alps merger: 9 months of preparation (self-reported; predates the Jan 2026 reports), 4 days to load the full data in production in mid-May 2026, and roughly 6 weeks of active follow-up (deck footnote: 'not precise ... at least 1.5 month').

- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 22 Text 104-172, 204 · "Codebase cleanup and documentation | 6 | Migrate Stacksync workload to Oban | 6 | Migrate local codebase and backups | 6 ... Four hygiene items have recurred for six weeks; none is closed"
- evidence: `projects/crm-three-scenarios/sources/aya-work2026-pptx-extract.txt` · SLIDE 14 Text 38-39 · "Unpaid hygiene debt | Four cleanup and backup items have recurred for six weeks. Closing them is cheap insurance for the pipeline above."
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] Headline sentence present verbatim: SLIDE 22 Text 204 (line 359) 'Four hygiene items have recurred for six weeks; none is closed', and SLIDE 14 Text 38-39 (lines 206-207) 'Unpaid hygiene debt' / 'Four cleanup and backup items have recurred for six weeks'. But the parenthetical '(cleanup, backups, codebase migration)' is the claimant's gloss: the deck never names the four. SLIDE 22 Text 104-172 shows only three six-week rows: 'Codebase cleanup and documentation' 6, 'Migrate Stacksync workload to Oban' 6, 'Migrate local codebase and backups' 6; the fourth is unidentified (SLIDE 7 Text 12 lists 'Misc: repo cleanup, codebase migration, backups' without counts). 'Six weeks' = six reported weeks; 'none is closed' is the author's status as of the 14 Sep report. → The deck states 'Four hygiene items have recurred for six weeks; none is closed' (as of the 14 Sep 2026 report); it names only three six-week rows (codebase cleanup and documentation, Stacksync-to-Oban migration, local codebase and backups) and does not identify the fourth; 'weeks' are reported weeks. [caveat] Sentence is verbatim on slide 22 Text 204 and echoed on slide 14 Text 39 ('Four cleanup and backup items have recurred for six weeks') and slide 19 Text 14. But the deck never enumerates the four. Recount of 6-week strips: slide 22 shows only three DevOps items at 6 (Codebase cleanup and documentation; Migrate Stacksync workload to Oban; Migrate local codebase and backups); other 6-week strips are on slide 20 (Knowledge base and CRM company cleanup) and slide 23 (Quarterly sales templates and report clean-up). 'Four' is reconcilable only by adding a Data or Process item, and the Oban migration is not cleanup/backups. The parenthetical '(cleanup, backups, codebase migration)' comes from BRIEF 2c / slide 7 'Misc: repo cleanup, codebase migration, backups', not from a deck enumeration. 'Six weeks' = 6 of 21 reported weeks; 'none closed' is as of the 14 Sep report. The debt story (V04/V13/V18) still follows; only the itemisation is unverified. → Deck reports four hygiene items (cleanup, backups, codebase migration) recurring for six of the 21 reported weeks, none closed as of 14 Sep 2026; the deck does not list the four - the visible 6-week strips are 'Codebase cleanup and documentation', 'Migrate local codebase and backups' and 'Migrate Stacksync workload to Oban' (slide 22) plus 'Knowledge base and CRM company cleanup' (slide 20).

Three rows are 'in progress' with 0 completed days (lines 14, 17, 22). Row-level PROGRESS values recompute correctly (7/13, 13/24, 3/17, 1/10, 14/44, 8/46). '0 complete' holds under both readings, which is what V11 needs. → Quote as: 'Tracker header reports 19 tasks, 0 complete (header: 5 in progress, 14 not started); the 13 task rows in the sheet show 10 in progress, 3 not started, 0 complete; the header was not recalculated after row statuses were filled.' ## C055

**Duplicate detection is exact-match on normalised name / domain / email (lowercase, whitesp…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[dp_duplicates]]

- kind: mapping · used by: V06, V21
- claim: Duplicate detection is exact-match on normalised name / domain / email (lowercase, whitespace collapsed) and duplicate_ids under-count for clusters larger than 10; the 226 / 281 clusters are a floor, not a count.
- evidence: `Hs-Logic/README.md` · line 74 · "Known limitation: `duplicate_ids` in both health payloads derives from cluster members truncated to 10 per cluster, so it under-counts for larger clusters."
- evidence: `Hs-Logic/backend/app/routes/hubspot.py` · lines 297-299, 315-318, 412-448 · "Companies are grouped by normalised domain (non-empty) and by normalised name. Any group with ≥ 2 members is a duplicate cluster. ... for c in group[:10]"
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] Quote is verbatim in Hs-Logic/README.md but at line 75, not 74 (line 74 is 'Refresh button in UI forces rescan, bypassing cache.'). Exact-match-after-normalisation confirmed in code: _normalize() lines 297-299 only lowercases/collapses whitespace; companies grouped by normalised domain/name (317-318, 416-417), contacts by normalised email/full name (531-532, 627). But the README limitation concerns the `duplicate_ids` ID list (truncated to 10 members per cluster), not the cluster COUNT. 'Clusters are a floor' holds for a different reason: the 10,000 cap and exact-match grouping can only under-detect. → Duplicate detection is exact-match on normalised (lowercased, whitespace-collapsed) name/domain for companies and email/full name for contacts. README.md line 75: `duplicate_ids` is truncated to 10 members per cluster, so the ID list under-counts for larger clusters. Cluster counts (226 / 281) are a floor because of the 10,000-record cap and exact-match grouping, not because of that truncation. [caveat] Exact-match on normalised keys is confirmed (_normalize = lowercase + collapse whitespace, lines 297-299; grouping by domain/name for companies 413-421, email/full name for contacts 531-532). The README quote is real but is on line 75, not 74. Logical slip: the quoted limitation concerns duplicate_ids (member list truncated to 10 per cluster, lines 441-446 and 660-668), NOT the cluster counts — 'count': len(group) is untruncated, so 226/281 clusters are not under-counted by that mechanism. Clusters are a floor only because of the 10,000 cap and exact matching (fuzzy duplicates missed); moreover a domain cluster and a name cluster with overlapping but non-identical membership are counted separately (seen_cluster_ids dedupes identical id-sets only, lines 424-432), so the cluster count is not a strict floor in every case. → Reword: 'Duplicate detection is exact-match on lowercased, whitespace-collapsed name/domain (companies) or email/full name (contacts), so fuzzy duplicates are missed and the scan stops at 10,000 records: the real duplicate volume is at least what is shown. Separately, the exported duplicate_ids list is truncated to 10 members per cluster (README line 75), so the ID list, not the cluster count, under-counts large clusters.'
- cited by views: V06, V21

Commit 17b363a message independently names where the deployment was: 'deployed understand.tech app (portal 9201667)'. → Hs-Logic runs via docker-compose on a local host (ports 127.0.0.1:8000 and 8080) plus a launch.json hard-coding one user's Windows path; a docker-compose comment ('broke the deployed app's /portal route once') and the seed commit ('extracted fragments from deployed understand.tech app') show a deployment existed, at understand.tech, not Cloud Run.

**The only write Hs-Logic performs is a suppression list (static HubSpot list from NQL ids; …** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[p_hs_logic]]

- evidence: `Hs-Logic/backend/app/routes/hubspot.py` · lines 723-735 · "Create a static HubSpot contact list and bulk-add the supplied contact IDs ... Required private-app scope: crm.lists.write ... On scope error (403) the frontend falls back to a CSV download automatically."

- caveat: [caveat] backend/app/routes/hubspot.py lines 721-735: '@router.post("/suppression-list")' docstring 'Create a static HubSpot contact list and bulk-add the supplied contact IDs ... Required private-app scope: crm.lists.write ... On scope error (403) the frontend falls back to a CSV download automatically.' Only HubSpot mutations in the backend are the POST /crm/v3/lists (line 750) and PUT memberships/add (line 780); the other client.post (line 52, hs_post) serves search endpoints, and no merge call exists. Frontend fallback confirmed at frontend/src/tabs/ContactHealthTab.tsx lines 89-106 (err.status === 403 → downloadCsv('nql-suppression.csv')). 'No execution evidence' is an absence claim: the repo holds no logs/fixtures, and HubSpot list objects are not queryable through the read-only HubSQL tool, so it could not be positively verified either way. → The only HubSpot write in Hs-Logic is POST /api/suppression-list (creates a static contact list and adds members; tags only, never merges); the frontend falls back to a CSV download on 403. Whether this endpoint was ever executed against the portal could not be verified with the available read-only tools. [caveat] Verified: the only mutating HubSpot calls in the backend are hubspot.py:750 (POST /crm/v3/lists, processingType MANUAL) and :780 (PUT .../memberships/add) inside create_suppression_list; hs_post at :77/:370/:391 are search (read) calls. grep 'merge' over backend/app returns nothing. CSV fallback on 403 is implemented in frontend/src/tabs/ContactHealthTab.tsx:89-106 (downloadCsv) and documented at hubspot.py:732 and README.md:31. No logs/list ids in the repo, so 'no execution evidence' is an absence of evidence, not proof. Wording issue: it adds contacts to a static list; it does not 'tag' (no property writes). → The only write Hs-Logic performs is creating a static (MANUAL) contact list and adding contact IDs to it (POST /crm/v3/lists + PUT memberships/add); no property updates, no merges. On 403 the frontend downloads a CSV instead. The repo holds no evidence the list endpoint was ever executed.

- evidence: `HubSpot-Ruler/Data-Toolkit/hubspot_crm_schema.json` · product_distribution_live[0..2] · "{'product': 'Unassigned', 'deals': 822}, {'product': 'INES', 'deals': 240}, {'product': 'VIC408', 'deals': 158}"

(822 deals today)'. Live re-run 2026-10-01: QVault TPM IOT = 32 (matches); prod___product_name_new_ IS NULL = 832 across all deals (822 at the 2026-09-14 capture), and only 84 when restricted to open deals (hs_is_closed = false), which is the filter used in the P36 example query. Breeze 2 vs HubSQL 32 as reported in Prompt_Resource.md (2026-09-14)'. [caveat] Prompt_Resource.md line 10 '-- 32 (live)', line 12 'Breeze free-text on the deal name returned 2', line 79 P36 '(822 deals today)'. hubspot_crm_schema.json product_distribution_live confirms Unassigned = 822 and its 36 groups sum to 1,960, so 822 is whole-portal, all statuses (42.0% of 1,960). The P36 example query filters hs_is_closed = false, so the figure must not be presented as 822 OPEN deals. The 2-vs-32 gap is a method difference (deal-name free text vs property filter), which does support the under-count story. → 822 of 1,960 deals (42%, whole portal, open and closed, capture 2026-09-14) have no prod___product_name_new_; Breeze free-text on the deal name returned 2 vs 32 by property filter for QVault TPM IOT.

(3 files, 3 insertions); e7e3f42 13:14:24 'Add files via upload' (removes ~$ontology_workbook_full.xlsx lock file). Numbers and date exact. (3 files, 3 insertions: .gitignore + removal of two ~$*.xlsx lock files, leaving 74 tracked files); e7e3f42 13:14:24 'Add files via upload' (binary, no stat). So the history is three commits within three minutes on one day, not literally 'a single upload'. Older-repo references verified: hermes-agent/README.md line 44 and docs/VPS-SETUP.md line 23 say 'git clone <this repo> && cd HB-Workflow' (a different repo name); ui-extension/README.md line 5 names branch 'feature/ui-extension-platform-2026.03'. → HubSpot-Ruler's entire GitHub history is three commits made within three minutes on 2026-09-25 (initial commit 76 files / 33,700 insertions, then removal of two Excel lock files, then 'Add files via upload'); hermes-agent docs still instruct 'cd HB-Workflow', an older repo name.

- caveat: [caveat] Data-Toolkit/Prompt_Resource.md §5 lines 177-190 = 14 bullets and hubspot_crm_schema.json hubsql_rules has 14 entries, including 'FROM — One object per query ... No JOIN / UNION / subqueries / CTEs' and 'Max 2 associated object types per query'. hermes-agent/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 180-181: 'CRM Search: 5 req/s account-wide, 200 results/page, 10,000-result ceiling per query' — this describes the CRM Search REST endpoint used by hs_api.py, not a HubSQL rule. Prompt_Resource.md line 224 template 'Do not match on dealname unless I say so'; line 29 rule R7: dealname LIKE is 'a LAST RESORT ... flagged approximate' — a restriction, not a prohibition. → Replace '10,000-result search ceiling, no dealname matching' with '10,000-result ceiling per CRM Search API query (hermes hs_api.py, not a HubSQL rule); dealname LIKE only as a flagged last resort (R7) and never for product/category counts'. [caveat] 14 rules verified twice: Data-Toolkit/Prompt_Resource.md §5 lines 177-190 has exactly 14 bullets (FROM 'One object per query ... No JOIN / UNION / subqueries / CTEs' line 177; 'Max 2 associated object types per query' line 186) and hubspot_crm_schema.json hubsql_rules is a list of 14. Two conflations: (1) the 10,000-result ceiling is not in the HubSQL cheat-sheet; it appears in hermes-agent/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 180-181 for the CRM Search REST API ('CRM Search: 5 req/s account-wide, 200 results/page, 10,000-result ceiling per query'), the same API behind Hs-Logic's cap, not query_crm_data/HubSQL. (2) 'no dealname matching' overstates: Prompt_Resource.md line 29 (R7) makes dealname LIKE a 'LAST RESORT ... flagged approximate', and the template line 224 says 'Do not match on dealname unless I say so'. → HubSQL practice: 14 cheat-sheet rules (one object per query, no JOIN/UNION/subqueries, max 2 associated object types); dealname matching is a flagged last resort, never for product counts. Separately, the CRM Search API (used by Hs-Logic and the hermes agent) stops at 10,000 results per query.

**In hermes-agent the S1 'Rules to comply' layer is prompt text, not enforcement: write guar…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[gate_rules_s1]]

- kind: mapping · used by: V16
- claim: In hermes-agent the S1 'Rules to comply' layer is prompt text, not enforcement: write guardrails (Notes/Tasks only, never delete or merge, property updates only on explicit instruction) live in SKILL.md files; the bridge enforces only a 64 KB body cap and unknown-action rejection in code.
- evidence: `HubSpot-Ruler/hermes-agent/skills/revops/hubspot-crm/SKILL.md` · lines 52-59 · "Default write-back channel is a Note ... Update properties ONLY when the triggering task explicitly asks for it. NEVER delete or merge records."
- evidence: `HubSpot-Ruler/hermes-agent/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` · line 111 · "Payloads | 64 KB body cap; unknown `action` rejected; `custom` requires explicit prompt"
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] hermes-agent/skills/revops/hubspot-crm/SKILL.md lines 52-59 'Write guardrails' contain verbatim 'Update properties ONLY when the triggering task explicitly asks for it.' and 'NEVER delete or merge records.' — prompt text. ARCHITECTURE.md line 111 'Payloads | 64 KB body cap; unknown action rejected; custom requires explicit prompt'. bridge/app.py lines 184-186 return 413 when len(raw_body) > config.MAX_BODY_BYTES (.env.example line 37 default 65536); lines 205-211 reject unknown action; lines 213-215 reject 'custom' without a prompt; lines 189-193 reject requests failing signature verification. 'Only' is overstated: code also enforces signatures and custom-needs-prompt, and hs_api.py exposes only get/search/associations/note/task/update subcommands (no delete/merge command). → Reword: 'code enforces a 64 KB body cap (default 65,536 bytes), unknown-action rejection, custom-needs-prompt, and request-signature checks; hs_api.py ships no delete/merge command; the Notes-only default, no-delete/merge and explicit-instruction-for-property-updates rules are SKILL.md prompt guardrails, not enforcement.' [caveat] hermes-agent/skills/revops/hubspot-crm/SKILL.md lines 52-59 '## Write guardrails' contain the quoted prose ('Update properties ONLY when the triggering task explicitly asks for it. NEVER delete or merge records.'), and nothing in code checks them: skills/revops/hubspot-crm/hs_api.py exposes an unguarded 'update' subcommand that PATCHes any property (lines 160-167, 206); no delete/merge subcommand exists, which is absence rather than enforcement. bridge/app.py does enforce the 64 KB cap (line 184, config.MAX_BODY_BYTES default 65536) and rejects unknown actions (lines 205-212). But 'only' is too narrow: the bridge also enforces request signatures with a 5-minute replay window (hermeslib/security.py lines 41-60, 86, 110; app.py 188-193) and requires a prompt for action 'custom' (app.py 213-215). → The hermes 'Rules to comply' are prompt text in SKILL.md, not enforcement; the code enforces transport-level checks only (request signature + 5-min replay window, 64 KB body cap, unknown-action rejection, prompt required for 'custom'), while hs_api.py's 'update' command can PATCH any property with no guard.

- caveat: [caveat] Re-ran live HubSQL 2026-10-01: final_customer IS NOT NULL AND associations.COMPANY IS NOT NULL = 927; final_customer IS NOT NULL AND associations.COMPANY IS NULL = 116; final_customer IS NOT NULL = 1043. Numbers exact. Scope is portal-wide (all 5 pipelines, 1,972 deals), which differs from the SEALSQ-only 931/1,820 (property set 2026-09-14) and the Hs-Logic screenshot 1,034. → Portal-wide, live HubSQL as of 2026-10-01 (1,972 deals across 5 pipelines): 1,043 deals carry final_customer; 927 also have an associated company (coexist), 116 do not. Not comparable 1:1 with the SEALSQ-only 931/1,820 (2026-09-14) or the Hs-Logic screenshot's 1,034. [caveat] Live re-run: FC NOT NULL AND COMPANY NOT NULL = 927; FC NOT NULL AND COMPANY NULL = 116; FC NOT NULL = 1,043; 927+116=1,043 exact. Scope: portal-wide (1,972 deals incl. Wisekey Services + PKI, which has 110 FC-filled deals), not the SEALSQ 1,820-deal subset (931 on 2026-09-14) nor the HS-Logic screenshot (1,034, capped scan). Logical gap: 'coexist' shows both a final_customer string and a company association exist, not that the associated company duplicates the final customer; in the sample the associated party is often the distributor (e.g. 'Fronius/Ineltek', 'Pow Technology / UK (Ineltek)'), so the number alone does not prove duplicate company creation for V09. → 927 deals (portal-wide, all pipelines, live 2026-10-01) carry BOTH a final_customer string and at least one associated company; 116 carry the string with no company at all (927+116=1,043). An associated company may be the distributor/sold-to rather than the final customer, so 927 is an upper bound on 'final customer duplicated as a company'. Do not mix with the 931/1,820 SEALSQ-subset figure (2026-09-14) or the 1,034 HS-Logic capped-scan figure.

Buckets sum to 13,679 (= live company total, so no null-createdate rows). 60.3% is derived: (4003+4250)/13679 = 0.6033. The 2026 bucket is year-to-date only. → 2026 figure (4,250) is a partial year, created 1 Jan–1 Oct 2026 (live HubSQL as of 2026-10-01); the 60.3% share is derived (8,253 / 13,679). Scope caveats for V10: 2026 is a partial year (1 Jan-1 Oct) and the 2026 bucket contains the Ic'Alps bulk load (BRIEF 2c: full data in production mid-May 2026), so createdate measures when records entered the portal, not organic growth; a year-on-year growth reading would conflate migration with acquisition. → Add: '2026 = 1 Jan-1 Oct only; 2025-26 counts include bulk-loaded Ic'Alps records (migrated mid-May 2026), so createdate reflects entry into the portal, not organic growth.'

## C116

**The HubSpot SQL probe has traps: COUNT(hs_object_id) is executed as a SUM of record IDs (1…** · type `evidence_fact` · depth 4 · parent [[p_hubsql_probe]]

- kind: mapping · used by: V16, V21
- claim: The HubSpot SQL probe has traps: COUNT(hs_object_id) is executed as a SUM of record IDs (18944500703385 for SealSQ Hardware vs 730 with COUNT(*)), and association filters are silently dropped in GROUP BY and some single-filter queries; only COUNT(*) results are used as facts.
- evidence: `live-hubsql-2026-10-01` · SELECT pipeline, COUNT(hs_object_id) FROM DEAL GROUP BY pipeline · "Pipeline [pipeline]	(Sum) Deal [hs_object_id]	SealSQ Hardware (12096408)	18944500703385"
- evidence: `live-hubsql-2026-10-01` · SELECT COUNT(hs_object_id) FROM DEAL WHERE associations.COMPANY IS NULL · "(Count) Deals [count] 1972"
- status: caveat (verified 2026-10-01)
- caveat: [caveat] Re-ran SELECT pipeline, COUNT(hs_object_id) FROM DEAL GROUP BY pipeline: header '(Sum) Deal [hs_object_id]', SealSQ Hardware (12096408) 18944500703385 — quote present verbatim. Also tested the GROUP BY trap: SELECT pipeline, COUNT(*) FROM DEAL WHERE associations.COMPANY IS NULL GROUP BY pipeline returned 730/303/140/1/798, identical to the unfiltered GROUP BY, so the association filter is silently dropped in GROUP BY. However the single-filter form SELECT COUNT(*) FROM DEAL WHERE associations.COMPANY IS NULL did honour the filter (160 vs 1,972), so 'some single-filter queries' is not reproduced by this test and has no locator. → Verified: COUNT(hs_object_id) aggregates as a SUM of IDs, and association filters (associations.COMPANY IS NULL) are ignored under GROUP BY. The plain single-filter count did honour the filter in a 2026-10-01 re-test (160 of 1,972 deals); keep 'some single-filter queries' hedged or drop it unless a specific failing query is cited. [caveat] Trap 1 reproduced live: SELECT pipeline, COUNT(hs_object_id) FROM DEAL GROUP BY pipeline returns '(Sum) Deal [hs_object_id]' with SealSQ Hardware = 18944500703385 (exact match); COUNT(*) gives 730. Trap 2 reproduced for GROUP BY: SELECT pipeline, COUNT(*) FROM DEAL WHERE associations.COMPANY IS NULL GROUP BY pipeline returns 730/303/140/1/798 = 1,972 (the whole table), so the association filter was silently dropped. But SELECT COUNT(*) FROM DEAL WHERE associations.COMPANY IS NULL returned 160, so 'some single-filter queries' dropping the filter could not be reproduced. 'Only COUNT(*) used' is a method statement not independently checkable. → Reword: 'association filters are silently dropped when combined with GROUP BY (verified 2026-10-01: grouped counts sum to the full 1,972); the plain single-filter COUNT(*) honoured the filter (160) in the verification run.'
- cited by views: V16, V21

- evidence: `https://ecosystem.hubspot.com/marketplace/apps/dedupely` · as of 2026-10-01, pricing tab · "Starter $32/month up to 30.000 records ... Basic up to 60.000 records ... Pro up to 120.000 ... Premium up to 240.000 ... Gold up to 480.000 records; All plans include all features"

- caveat: [caveat] Dedupely listing confirmed: 'Starter $32/month up to 30.000 records'; Basic 60.000 / Pro 120.000 / Premium 240.000 / Gold 480.000 shown with '7 day free trial' and no monthly price. The 39,972 figure is NOT on the cited source: it is contacts+companies from an earlier live probe (26,293+13,679); my read-only HubSQL re-run on 2026-10-01 gives 26,294+13,679 = 39,973. Sibling claim C128 uses 41,944 (adds 1,972 deals) for the same portal, so the record basis is inconsistent across claims, and the listing does not define which objects count as 'records'. → Add a second evidence entry: source live-hubsql, locator 'SELECT COUNT(*) FROM CONTACT / COMPANY, 2026-10-01', quote 'contacts 26,294; companies 13,679'. Word as: 'portal contacts + companies ~39,970 (live 2026-10-01: 26,294 + 13,679 = 39,973; counts drift daily) already exceed the 30,000-record Starter cap; which objects Dedupely counts as records is not stated on the listing.' Live HubSQL COUNT(*) re-run: CONTACT 26,294 + COMPANY 13,679 = 39,973; the claimed 39,972 is the earlier same-day capture (26,293 + 13,679). Conclusion (exceeds Starter's 30,000, fits Basic's 60,000) holds, and still holds if Dedupely counts deals too (1,972 -> 41,944). Dedupely's definition of 'records' and the Basic price are not on the listing. → Dedupely Starter USD 32/month covers up to 30,000 records; the portal's 13,679 companies + 26,29x contacts (~39,97x, live 2026-10-01, moves with inbound creation) exceed Starter, so the Basic tier (60,000) would apply — its price is not shown on the listing and Dedupely does not state which object types count as records.

[caveat] Page re-fetched: five tiers 'Solutions partner, gold, platinum, diamond, and elite'; tiering by sourced points, total points and average GRR, but GRR thresholds exist only for Diamond (>=75%) and Elite (>=80%), N/A for Gold/Platinum. The '$400/month partner program membership' takes effect July 15, 2026 and is WAIVED when net product subscription is >= $400/month after discounts. The page gives 'January 2027' as the start of performance-requirement enforcement; a specific '2027-01-15' date was not found in the fetched text. → Solutions Partner tiers: five tiers (Solutions partner, Gold, Platinum, Diamond, Elite) set by sourced and total points, with a GRR floor only at Diamond (75%) and Elite (80%); USD 400/month membership from 15 Jul 2026, waived when the partner's net HubSpot subscription is >= USD 400/month; performance enforcement from January 2027 — thresholds subject to change.

- caveat: [caveat] cost-lines.md line 67, S3 table row 'Overlap with StackSync', contains the quote verbatim: 'Data Hub Professional USD 720 / month **in addition to** StackSync USD 1,000–3,000 / month annual list for as long as both are kept'. The claim is faithful, but the source row and the file header qualify the figures as public list prices on annual billing (Data Hub Pro USD 800 monthly; StackSync USD 1,400 / 3,700 month-to-month), as-of 2026-10-01, and the header states the team's actual StackSync plan and HubSpot tier are not in the repos. → Public list prices (annual billing) as of 2026-10-01 from hubspot.com/pricing/data and stacksync.com/pricing; the portal's current HubSpot tier and actual StackSync plan are not recorded in the repos, so this is an exposure estimate, not a measured delta. [caveat] Figures verified live on 2026-10-01: hubspot.com/pricing/data shows Data Hub Professional 'Starts at $720/mo' (annual) / '$800/mo' monthly; stacksync.com/pricing shows Starter $1,000/mo and Pro $3,000/mo billed annually. Sum = USD 1,720-3,720 per month (USD 20,640-44,640/yr). Scope issue: both are list prices; the team's actual StackSync plan is not in the repos and the portal's Data Hub tier is recorded nowhere (live read-only org probe returns seat types only, no hub tier), so 'in addition to' is a list-price ceiling, not a measured incremental cost. The range also omits StackSync Managed Pro (USD 4,200/mo). → Overlap at list price: Data Hub Professional USD 720/month (annual) on top of StackSync USD 1,000-3,000/month (Starter/Pro, annual) while both run, i.e. USD 1,720-3,720/month; the actual StackSync plan and the portal's current HubSpot tier are not recorded, so this is a ceiling, not a delta.
